Its Claim Receipt and Surface Transcript declaration gaps begin at the contract horizon, the commit that added the concrete-contract guide to the write-techspec skill. It reports consistency findings and never edits artifacts or emits a QA verdict. Its Glossary Declaration gap begins at the glossary horizon, the commit that added the glossary guide to the write-prd skill, and a Spec that carries a Glossary Declaration is checked at any age.
_Avoid_: QA gate, Spec validator, inference engine

**Claim Receipt**:
A source-and-quote pair in the same paragraph as an attribution to a decision record. The Spec Consistency Check proves that the verbatim quote occurs in the named source after whitespace normalization; it does not judge whether the quote supports the claim.
_Avoid_: Citation proof, supported claim

**Surface Transcript**:
A numbered TechSpec declaration of one command surface containing its command, standard output, standard error, and exit code. The QA gate reproduces it through the built product and compares the streams using the transcript's exact-match and controlled-variation conventions.
_Avoid_: CLI example, expected output prose

**Consistency Finding Severity**:
Each Spec Consistency Check finding is an `error` when the check locates both sides of a contradiction, or a `gap` when it surfaces a candidate it cannot settle. The `SC-*` diagnostic codes are stable and never renumbered once shipped.
_Avoid_: QA verdict, priority, confidence

**Metric Declaration Gap (`SC-METRIC-UNDECLARED`)**:
The Spec Consistency Check gap raised when a Spec's `_prd.md` does not declare numbered Success Metrics or an explicit `None.` with a reason.
_Avoid_: Missing metric, untraced metric

**Contract Declaration Gap (`SC-CONTRACT-UNDECLARED`)**:
The Spec Consistency Check gap raised when a Spec's `_techspec.md` does not declare numbered API Contracts or an explicit `None.` with a reason.
_Avoid_: Missing contract, untraced contract

**Live Document**:
A tracked Markdown file inside an active Spec folder, an open Backlog Entry, an unresolved Finding, or under `docs/references/`; a citation is a relative link destination or repository-relative path naming a file removed by retirement (ADR-0260).
_Avoid_: Citing file, live source, current document

**Archive Relink**:
The Archive Command's pre-write operation that promotes a cited non-core file to `docs/references/<basename>`, rewrites Live Document citations to the promoted copy or Archive Record, and records rewritten paths under the optional `relinked` list (ADR-0260).
_Avoid_: Archive rewrite, source duplication, link cleanup

**History Citation**:
A citation from tracked Markdown outside the History Root to a file or folder a selected history unit removes, which `roundfix baseline update` lists in text and JSON, binds into the Plan Digest, and may offer for `--promote` when it is a Live Document (ADR-0260).
_Avoid_: Relocation Citation, broken-link scan, stale citation

**Stale History Citation**:
A citation already unresolved in the working tree whose target remains in the History Full Tag, which `roundfix baseline update` reports with a `git show history-full:<path>` restore command outside the Plan Digest and update state (ADR-0260).
_Avoid_: Broken link, history warning, missing source

**Archive Record**:
The small `<slug>.md` record an archive leaves under the resolved archive root: a file named `NNNN-<slug>.md` whose front matter declares `roundfix/archive-record/v1`; any other Markdown file in that root is not a record. It carries the disposition, QA Report and verdict, promoted references, `source_revision` and, once the delivery pull request is open, `pull_request`; the removed Spec folder stays recoverable in Git at that revision, after a squash merge through Source Revision Recovery. The History Sanitize Command also writes one for a Legacy Archive Folder; a folder without a QA Report receives the disposition `no-qa`, while a folder whose newest QA Report failed without an override receives `failed-qa` and keeps that verdict and report name. `failed-qa` is never a pass or an override (ADR-0248, ADR-0251, ADR-0260, ADR-0261). A cancelled Spec's record has the disposition `cancelled` and carries `cancel_reason`, `completed_tasks`, `pending_tasks` and `returned_sources`; only its listed completed Tasks count as completed (ADR-0262).
_Avoid_: Archive folder, archive log, copied Spec

**Folder Removal Recovery**:
How the Archive Command and the Cancel Command end when the final removal of the Spec folder fails: every write stays in place, and the command prints the command that finishes the removal (ADR-0269).
_Avoid_: Rollback, retry archive, partial cleanup

**Archive Advice**:
Jev's advisory classification of candidate files before the archive cut. It labels reusable knowledge, repository records or transient evidence and never gates the archive.
_Avoid_: Archive verdict, promotion decision, required evidence

**Archive Command**:
The support command that performs an Archive Relink, then retires a Spec under the archive eligibility contract, writes its Archive Record, and removes the Spec folder while keeping it in Git at `source_revision`. It warns when `source_revision` is not an ancestor of the default branch, and `--pull-request <n>` stores the delivery pull request in the record, also in a record the current branch already added (ADR-0261). Normal QA eligibility and a user-authorized QA Archive Override are distinct dispositions. Neither retirement nor an override fabricates Task completion or a passing QA verdict (ADR-0260).
_Avoid_: Move command, retire run, cleanup command

**Source Revision Recovery**:
A reader's fetch of `refs/pull/<N>/head` when an Archive Record's `source_revision` is missing locally, with the number taken from the record's `pull_request` or from the trailing `(#N)` of the commit that added the record. It writes no ref, never prompts, and leaves the reader's unavailable outcome when it fails (ADR-0261).
_Avoid_: Backfill, archive ref, history restore

**Pull Request Record Commit**:
The Delivery Queue's commit, made after it opens a Spec's pull request, that stores the number in the Archive Record its archive commit added and changes nothing else; the queue accepts it by proof instead of a second repository gate (ADR-0261).
_Avoid_: Archive commit, record backfill, amend

**History Sanitize Command**:
The `roundfix history sanitize` command, a dry run unless `--apply --batch <n>`, that converts the existing history after the History Full Tag. `roundfix baseline update` runs the same planning for every convertible unit at once, while this command remains the path for reviewed batches (ADR-0254).
_Avoid_: Archive Command, history deletion, cleanup command

**Pending History**:
The units of a repository's existing history that the History Sanitize Command would plan, which the Managed Refresh includes in its plan and the upgrade notice names. `roundfix baseline update` plans these units with the Baseline Plan (ADR-0254).
_Avoid_: Active history, current history, unplanned archive

**Legacy Archive Folder**:
A Spec folder the Archive Command left under the archive root before archives wrote Archive Records (ADR-0248).
_Avoid_: Archive Record, active Spec, history root

**Refused Unit**:
A pending History Sanitize Command unit that cannot be converted. The plan and the batch name it with its reason on one line, leave it untouched, and do not count it toward `--batch <n>`. The update lists one without blocking the Baseline Plan or changing its state; a unit with uncommitted changes is refused (ADR-0251, ADR-0254).
_Avoid_: Converted unit, skipped silently, failed batch

**Lenient Legacy Reading**:
The reading of a Legacy Archive Folder's Task Graph that tolerates and names projection rows outside the graph and retired Task types. It also accepts a legacy list of maps `unproven`, one text line per map. It is never applied to an active Spec (ADR-0251, ADR-0254).
_Avoid_: Relaxed active reading, manifest rewrite, schema bypass

**Sanitize Batch**:
The next units one `--apply` can convert, delivered as one Pull Request with the repository gates green and revertible; Refused Units do not count toward the batch. The update converts every convertible unit as one batch in its own change (ADR-0248, ADR-0251, ADR-0254).
_Avoid_: Archive batch, migration wave, unreviewed cleanup

**Reduced History Entry**:
A retired Finding or Backlog Entry cut to its front matter, title, first paragraph and the revision holding its full text. A retirement writes it directly, and the History Sanitize Command reduces the entries retired before (ADR-0248, ADR-0258).
_Avoid_: Summary, Archive Record, truncated history

**History Full Tag**:
The annotated `history-full` tag on the last commit before the first batch, which keeps every removed byte reachable. The update creates it at `HEAD` when absent, never moves it, and the operator pushes it with `git push origin history-full` (ADR-0248, ADR-0254).
_Avoid_: Lightweight tag, batch tag, archive stamp

**History Root**:
The single repository directory holding Archive Records, Reduced History Entries and retired ADRs, with the full bytes in Git. Retired material leaves the directory an Agent loads by default and stays greppable as the record of what was built and why; one path filter excludes the whole tree from review. Resolved in exactly one place, so no consumer carries a path of its own (ADR-0248).
_Avoid_: Archive folder, `_archived`, attic, trash

**History Relocation**:
One retired file that belongs under the History Root and is not there, carried in the Baseline Plan as an ordered ledger entry of source, destination, and content identity rather than file bytes. A relocation whose destination is occupied is a collision: it is refused by name, its siblings still move, and the repository is not current until it is resolved.
_Avoid_: Archive move, file migration, relocation payload

**Relocation Citation**:
A citation in a tracked file that resolves before a Baseline Plan's History Relocations and would not resolve after them. It is reported as a warning the Plan Digest binds; planning never rewrites it, and apply writes the same files.
_Avoid_: Broken link, dangling reference, link check

**Worktree Build Cache**:
A repository-declared directory of rebuildable build output retained in a Run Worktree, Task Worktree or Delivery Queue item worktree. It becomes reclaimable when its Run is terminal or its item is merged; active work keeps it. See [ADR-0266](docs/adr/0266-a-signal-goes-only-to-a-proven-process-and-worktrees-share-one-build-cache.md).
_Avoid_: Run artifacts, shared build cache

**GC Command**:
The support command that reclaims Run storage: it prunes the Run Event Journal and artifact directory of terminal Runs older than the Journal Retention window, removes orphaned run artifact directories, runs the Run Retention Sweep without a budget, removes declared Worktree Build Caches from ended worktrees, and reports what it freed. See [ADR-0266](docs/adr/0266-a-signal-goes-only-to-a-proven-process-and-worktrees-share-one-build-cache.md). A Run it already emptied is not counted again. It never touches Active Runs or active-run locks, and it removes a `runs` row only through Run Retention (ADR-0255).
_Avoid_: Clean command, vacuum, purge

**Journal Retention**:
The configured age window after which a terminal Run's Run Event Journal and artifact directory become eligible for pruning. Active Runs are never eligible; a retention of zero keeps everything. See ADR-0033. It is the inner window: Run Retention removes the whole Run past the outer window, so a Journal Retention longer than the Run Retention has no effect past it (ADR-0255).
_Avoid_: Log rotation, TTL, expiry

**Run Retention**:
The User Config window, `store.run_retention_days` of 7, 15 or 30 days and 30 by default, after which a terminal Run leaves the Run Database whole: its row, its Run Event Journal, its Agent Selection records, its token usage and its artifact directory. A Run that is not terminal, that a Delivery Queue references, whose Run Worktree still exists, or whose artifact directory sits under an unproven root is kept (ADR-0255).
_Avoid_: Journal Retention, TTL, purge

**Run Retention Sweep**:
The step that applies Run Retention and then returns freed pages to the filesystem by incremental compaction. It runs at most once a day at the start of a Run or a Delivery Queue under a two-second budget, continuing at the next start when the budget runs out, and without a budget in the GC Command (ADR-0255).
_Avoid_: cleanup job, cron, vacuum

**Worktree Bootstrap**:
The configured command Roundfix runs once in a newly created Run or Task Worktree, after copying `worktree.copy` files and before Agent work and Verification, to prepare the environment (install dependencies, migrate and seed databases, warm caches). A bootstrap failure ends the Run or settles the Task with a bootstrap-failed outcome. See ADR-0034.
_Avoid_: Setup command, provisioning, install step

**Context-Driven Baseline**:
The portable, versioned set of required project instructions and architectural decisions that makes a repository ready for the CONTEXT-driven workflow.
_Avoid_: Sample docs, optional setup, repository template

**Baseline Profile**:
A versioned composition of modules, Repository Capabilities, decisions, managed artifacts, and verification roles that defines one reproducible Context-Driven Baseline. Roundfix ships built-in profiles; a repository may own additional profiles for its own workflow.
_Avoid_: Agent Selection Profile, setup template, user profile

**Profile Draft**:
A strict repository-owned Baseline Profile document supplied to planning in memory and admitted only after catalog validation and source binding.
_Avoid_: Partial profile, unchecked JSON, Profile override

**Profile Adaptation**:
A maintainer-reviewed Profile Draft that narrows one built-in Baseline Profile by removing repository-inapplicable modules or Repository Capabilities without weakening universal requirements.
_Avoid_: Profile waiver, capability bypass, silent inference

**Baseline Command**:
The public `roundfix baseline` command that authors and validates Baseline Profiles and drives one preflight-to-verification adoption or update flow without requiring an Agent Skill.
_Avoid_: Setup Command, setup skill, configuration wizard

**Source Baseline**:
The immutable Setup Manifest and governed instruction corpus that a setup transition or Baseline Readoption recognizes as its exact origin.
_Avoid_: Legacy Baseline, inferred preimage, current files

**Source Baseline Entry**:
One byte-evidenced structural unit in a Source Baseline that Baseline Readoption classifies and disposes individually as a Normative Clause, recommendation, Operational Contract, or non-governed evidence.
_Avoid_: Inferred rule, category summary, untracked paragraph

**Segmentation Snapshot**:
The immutable, checkout-free semantic-analysis input containing one Source Baseline and the strict byte-range proposal contract.
_Avoid_: Repository prompt, mutable instruction dump, checkout context

**Segmentation Proposal**:
A digest-bound, byte-exhaustive set of ordered ranges that splits Source Baseline Entries without rewriting or omitting source bytes.
_Avoid_: Summary, rewritten rules, partial range list

**Analysis Snapshot**:
The immutable, checkout-free classification input containing segmented Source Baseline Entries and only the semantic destinations active for the confirmed Baseline Profile and project decisions.
_Avoid_: Repository scan, unrestricted prompt, inferred destination list

**Classification Proposal**:
A digest-bound disposition for every entry in one Analysis Snapshot, admitted only after deterministic validation and explicit human review.
_Avoid_: Classification hint, partial proposal, autonomous decision

**Baseline Readoption**:
The confirmation-gated adoption that inventories an incompatible repository state as a Source Baseline and replaces its setup identity without treating existing instructions as disposable.
_Avoid_: Clean install, legacy fallback, automatic overwrite

**Normative Clause**:
An identified repository instruction whose enforcement is mandatory, prohibited, or stop-and-ask.
_Avoid_: Hard rule, guideline, free-form prose

**Normative Clause Manifest**:
The digest-bound inventory that independently accounts for a Source Baseline's Normative Clauses, recommendations, and Operational Contracts.
_Avoid_: Transition mapping, self-declared ledger, category checklist

**Operational Contract**:
An identified structured instruction whose order or shape carries required behavior, such as a template, procedure, or decision matrix.
_Avoid_: Prose summary, optional example, compressed guidance

**Instruction Hierarchy**:
The precedence order from universal instructions through context and documentation, Spec workflow, enabled autonomous work, stack and surface guides, and optional knowledge sources. A narrower guide may add constraints but cannot weaken a universal Normative Clause or confirmed project decision.
_Avoid_: Module list, arbitrary file order, duplicated root policy

**Standard TypeScript Monorepo Profile**:
The opinionated, project-agnostic Context-Driven Baseline profile for the repository's standard TypeScript monorepo stack.
_Avoid_: Project-specific profile, generic TypeScript profile, sample template

**Repository Capability**:
A profile-declared skill, tool, dependency, workspace, or repository contract whose required, recommended, or optional status is evaluated from explicit local evidence.
_Avoid_: Assumed stack, installed package list, inferred readiness

**Skill Activation**:
A declarative mapping from one stable work trigger to one exact required Agent Skill bundle, optionally conditioned on a selected Repository Capability.
_Avoid_: Suggested skill, inferred bundle, partial activation

**Decision Plan**:
The resolved setup proposal produced after every required setup decision has an answer; it is the basis for authorizing setup changes.
_Avoid_: Setup questionnaire, decision draft, configuration prompt

**Change Plan**:
The exact set of repository changes proposed for explicit review and confirmation before a setup or restoration operation mutates files.
_Avoid_: Patch, implicit apply, change preview

**Setup Manifest**:
The setup-owned record of the selected profile, modules, decisions, and managed artifacts that reproduces and audits a repository's Context-Driven Baseline. Every applied Baseline change republishes it, so its recorded digests describe the bytes on disk rather than the bytes adoption wrote.
_Avoid_: Project Config, lock file, generated guide

**Managed Region**:
The byte range one setup-owned marker pair delimits inside an instruction carrier, identified by its managed artifact identity. Its content belongs to the Context-Driven Baseline, not to the repository holding it; the bytes outside every marker pair belong to the repository and no Baseline operation changes them.
_Avoid_: Managed file, generated block, setup section

**Managed Refresh**:
The preservation mode that regenerates only Managed Regions, leaves every other byte identical, requires no Source Baseline and no Decision Plan, and takes no root backup because the plan's preimages carry the preservation proof. It also lists History Citations, binds them into the Plan Digest, accepts repeatable `--promote` values, and converts Pending History on approval. Its preservation proof still covers the instruction carriers. It converges: a second run against an unchanged catalog reports the repository current and proposes no change (ADR-0254, ADR-0260).
_Avoid_: Baseline Readoption, reapply, overwrite

**Unrecorded Managed Region**:
A Managed Region whose bytes are not the bytes the adopted Setup Manifest recorded. It is classified and reported by path, managed identity, and the lines a refresh removes — never presumed damaged and never silently replaced. Only a managed identity appearing more than once in one carrier blocks, because that refresh has no defensible target.
_Avoid_: Hand-edited marker, corrupted block, drift

**Baseline ADR**:
An Architecture Decision Record whose reserved identity and invariant belong to the Context-Driven Baseline while project-specific notes remain repository-owned.
_Avoid_: Example ADR, project ADR, template copy

**Upgrade Retention Contract**:
The accounting a Context-Driven Baseline transition or Baseline Readoption must satisfy before mutation: every Source Baseline Normative Clause, recommendation, and Operational Contract maps to a current managed target, Repository-Specific Normative Rules, a recognized typed repository document, or an explicit rejection with a recorded reason.
_Avoid_: Best-effort migration, category coverage, silent rule removal

**Repository-Specific Normative Rules**:
Project-authored Normative Clauses that are not portable across the Context-Driven Baseline and cannot be represented by a typed project decision or managed semantic guide. Baseline assigns every representable rule to its semantic owner; only non-empty residual rules live outside setup markers in `docs/agents/specific-repository.md` and remain byte-preserved after confirmed adoption.
_Avoid_: Repository-Owned Extension, duplicate managed rule, baseline rule

**HTTP Contract Decision**:
The repository-owned choice of REST or POST-only application API semantics together with explicit protocol or operational exceptions and their owners.
_Avoid_: HTTP profile, universal REST rule, inferred route style

**Better Auth Base Path**:
The path under which a repository mounts Better Auth's routes, the value of Better Auth's `basePath` option, recorded as the optional `auth.base_path` decision. When it is recorded, the provider's route exception and the matching HTTP Contract Decision exception cover that path followed by `/*`; when it is absent, the recorded exception stands, and Better Auth's own default is `/api/auth` ([ADR-0263](docs/adr/0263-the-better-auth-base-path-is-a-repository-decision-a-baseline-update-records.md)).
_Avoid_: Auth prefix, API prefix, auth scope

**Formatter-Stable Output**:
Generated managed Markdown that the target repository's selected formatter leaves unchanged, so apply, formatting, Verification, audit, and reapply compose with no delta.
_Avoid_: Renderer-canonical output, format-after-apply fixup

**Format Command**:
The Project Config command `verification.format` that the Daemon runs, outside the Agent sandbox, over the files under a Spec's `qa/` directory before the repository Verification precondition (an imported pass) and before the QA Report commit, restoring the original bytes when it fails (ADR-0249).
_Avoid_: Repository Verification, Baseline formatter, whole-tree formatter

**Late Dependency**:
A Task in a completed QA gate's dependency closure that the Task Graph did not hold when the newest QA Report was committed; `roundfix reopen` returns the gate to `pending` over it whatever its status (ADR-0249).
_Avoid_: Stale dependency, missing dependency, unresolved dependency

**Internal Identifier**:
A technical identity for an entity or resource that is generated and controlled by the project.
_Avoid_: External identifier, natural key, business code

**Roundfix Skill**:
A shipped agent skill that teaches an external Agent how to start Roundfix or how to resolve one assigned Batch.
_Avoid_: Runtime, Review Source, plugin

**Repository Skill Set**:
The complete set of Roundfix-owned and externally managed Agent Skills required by a repository workflow. Every required installed artifact matches its authoritative local source; unrelated extras do not join the set.
_Avoid_: Installed skills, plugin set, global skill set

**Vendored Skill**:
A skill a repository installs from an upstream source and never edits locally, because the next sync replaces its folder. A divergence is corrected in order: a rule that governs over the skill's default, then a repository-authored skill that supplies only what is missing, then a repository-authored replacement only when the vendored skill is useless or almost useless; an objective defect goes upstream as a pull request ([ADR-0264](docs/adr/0264-roundfix-never-replaces-a-vendored-skill-it-cannot-prove-older.md)).
_Avoid_: Forked skill, local skill copy, edited upstream skill

**Setup Snapshot Difference**:
A required external skill that is installed and matches its `skills-lock.json` entry but whose tree differs from the `treeDigest` its Setup Snapshot pins. Nothing offline tells whether the copy is newer or older, so Doctor names it without a warning and the managed refresh keeps it; only a deliberate per-skill restore replaces it ([ADR-0264](docs/adr/0264-roundfix-never-replaces-a-vendored-skill-it-cannot-prove-older.md)).
_Avoid_: Trailing skill, outdated skill, stale skill

**Interactive Input**:
The TUI flow that collects command parameters before a Run starts.
_Avoid_: Wizard, form, setup screen

**Live Run View**:
The TUI view that shows Review Issues and Run Events for a Run: streaming live while the Run is active, or replayed from the Run Event Journal during Attach.
_Avoid_: Dashboard, report, log file

**Run Browser**:
The read-only, machine-wide TUI list for Run discovery: every repository's Runs with their state, kind, target, Agent, start, duration, branch, and repository — Active Runs by default — from which selecting a Run opens the Live Run View through Attach.
_Avoid_: Run picker, run manager, dashboard

**Cockpit**:
The shared Live Run View composition made of the Phase Row, Work Queue, Session Timeline, footer, and optional Detail Modal.
_Avoid_: Dashboard, reduced pane

**Work Queue**:
The Live Run View pane that lists a Run's Work Items and their current status for both review and spec Runs.
_Avoid_: Issue list, task pane

**Phase Row**:
The Live Run View row that shows a Run's lifecycle position with terminal-text status markers.
_Avoid_: Progress header, pipeline bar

**Session Timeline**:
The Live Run View pane that shows grouped Run Events from the Run Event Journal.
_Avoid_: Console log, agent output pane

**Detail Modal**:
The centered Live Run View overlay that shows one selected Work Item's Review Issue artifact or Task file body read-only.
_Avoid_: Detail pane, inspector panel

**Daemon**:
The Roundfix process that owns the Run lifecycle and Review Source-facing outcomes.
_Avoid_: Orchestrator, controller, manager

**Run Event**:
One ordered product record of something meaningful that happened during a Run, carrying Run identity, Batch when known, event source, event kind, and a structured payload. Producers convert their native models into Run Events; ACP stream updates remain an Agent-internal protocol model.
_Avoid_: Stream update, log line, message

**Run Event Journal**:
The append-only history of Run Events stored in the Run Database, ordered by a per-Run cursor so replay is deterministic and duplicate-free.
_Avoid_: Agent log, log file, event broker

**Run Event Stream**:
A read-only JSONL projection of one Run's Run Event Journal, selected by an explicit Run ID and optionally followed until the Run reaches a terminal outcome. Its stable Supervisor filters are task status, Batch boundary, Verification verdict, and terminal outcome. The command skips a record it cannot project with a warning and continues with the next journal entry.
_Avoid_: Attach, console log, global event bus

**Attach**:
Viewing a Run by replaying its Run Event Journal and then following new Run Events, without owning, mutating, or stopping the Run.
_Avoid_: Resume, reconnect, takeover

**Agent**:
The local coding assistant invoked by Roundfix to triage and resolve an assigned Batch.
_Avoid_: Review Source, review provider, worker, bot

**Supervisor**:
The external Claude Code role that authors Specs, starts and monitors Runs, and delegates every Work Item to an Agent.
_Avoid_: Fable, Agent, ACP Runtime, Daemon, Orchestrator

**Follow Mode**:
The Live Run View state in which the timeline tail advances automatically as new Run Events arrive; suspended while the user scrolls back, resumed when the viewport returns to the bottom. Scrolling never affects the Run.
_Avoid_: tail mode, auto-scroll, live mode


**Pre-PR Review Policy**:
The repository's choice of Codex, Claude, CodeRabbit, or no reviewer before a
Pull Request is opened. An enabled review covers the current candidate, while
explicit none records an intentional omission that leaves QA and required
checks binding; provider failure is not an omission.
_Avoid_: Review mode, reviewer profile, automatic review

**Pre-PR Review Provider**:
The selected agent or service that performs review under the Pre-PR Review
Policy. An Agent Selection Profile supplies an agent provider's runtime and
model; explicit none is a policy choice with no provider.
_Avoid_: Review Source, Pre-PR Review Policy, reviewer model

**Pre-PR Review Record**:
The record of one Pre-PR Review, kept per checkout under its resolved path. Its
verdict is read from the reviewer's final message. See
[ADR-0272](docs/adr/0272-a-review-record-survives-a-fix-and-a-correction-inside-the-archived-specs-declared-paths-returns-to-review.md).
_Avoid_: Review Source Evidence, shared review state, reviewer transcript

**Reviewed Head Record**:
The copy of a Pre-PR Review Record kept under the head it reviewed, written for
every verdict that is not a reuse and never replaced by a later round at
another head. A Delivery Retry's correction proof reads it for the parked
candidate. See
[ADR-0272](docs/adr/0272-a-review-record-survives-a-fix-and-a-correction-inside-the-archived-specs-declared-paths-returns-to-review.md).
_Avoid_: round record, review history


**QA Archive Override**:
An explicit user request or applicable prior authorization to archive a covered
Spec despite its QA prerequisite being unmet. The recorded exception preserves
actual QA and Task evidence while retaining non-QA completion, source integrity
and separate delivery gates.
_Avoid_: QA pass, QA bypass, Implement Clean

**Glossary Declaration**:
The `## Glossary` section of a Spec's PRD or TechSpec that lists each domain term the Spec adds to or changes in the glossary, and each bolded phrase it declares not a domain term, or `None.`. A Task that declares the glossary file and names the term in its Verification writes each listed term.
_Avoid_: Vocabulary Contract, term list, glossary candidates

**Glossary Gap**:
A domain term a Spec introduces that the glossary does not carry as required: a bolded phrase the glossary lacks and the Glossary Declaration does not cover, a declared term no Task plans to write, or a declared term still missing after its Tasks completed. The Spec Consistency Check reports it, and the QA gate and the Archive Command refuse a Spec that has one.
_Avoid_: Missing term, undocumented token, stale glossary

**Light Tier**:
The dispatch tier that runs a Task on an open model through OpenCode and OpenRouter when its complexity is low, its type is not qa, and it declares no Governed Path; every other Task runs on the standard tier. A light Task whose first Verification fails escalates its one feedback turn to its category's Preferred Selection.
_Avoid_: Cheap model, Jev routing, light model profile

**Light Spend Log**:
The per-UTC-month record in Roundfix Home of the cost each Light Tier session reports, summed across every repository and compared with the Light Tier's monthly ceiling. A month that reached the ceiling, or whose log cannot be read, runs light Tasks on their category's profile.
_Avoid_: Judge Log, OpenRouter usage, spend cache

**Jev Ceiling**:
The monthly spending ceiling of the Jev judge, read from User Config only and compared with the Judge Log's sum across every repository on the machine; when it is unset a built-in default applies. The judge's key must carry a monthly credit limit no higher than it.
_Avoid_: Judge budget, key limit, Project Config ceiling

**Stage Key**:
The Roundfix environment variable an OpenRouter stage reads first, the judge's or implementation's own key, before the shared Roundfix OpenRouter key; the generic OpenRouter variable is never read. Each stage records the variable it used by name, never by value.
_Avoid_: API key value, generic OpenRouter key, shared key

**Tested Base**:
The default-branch commit a failed required check actually tested, read from the check job's annotation. A failure whose Tested Base does not contain the current default-branch tip is stale, and the Delivery Queue re-runs it instead of parking the item.
_Avoid_: PR base, merge base, Delivery Base

**Merge Evidence**:
Proof that a Spec was delivered: the default-branch head holds its archived PRD, and the Delivery Commit that added that file is not reachable from the Run Branch or Item Branch being proven. With it, reconcile releases that Spec's terminal Runs and Item Branches without comparing content.
_Avoid_: Content proof, merged flag, merge commit

**Delivery Commit**:
The default-branch commit that added a Spec's archived PRD. Merge Evidence and a Delivery Retry that records a merge made outside the queue both read it.
_Avoid_: Merge commit, archive commit, release commit

**Item Branch**:
The branch a Delivery Queue item builds its candidate on, named `roundfix/deliver-<slug>-<16 hex>`. Once no live item names it, Merge Evidence releases it, with its worktree only when that worktree is clean.
_Avoid_: Run Branch, PR Head Branch, deliver branch

**Network-Denied Row**:
An outside-evidence QA row blocked only because the Run sandbox denied network access, recorded with the host it could not reach. Like the pre-PR Pull Request row, it never decides a qualifying partial; whoever needs that proof declares it under Unreachable Acceptance.
_Avoid_: Failed outside evidence, skipped row, environment pass

**Pre-PR Review Command**:
The command that obtains the Pre-PR Review Provider's review of the current candidate before a Pull Request opens, reads the verdict from the reviewer's final message and keeps the Pre-PR Review Record. A finding parks delivery only after it is validated.
_Avoid_: Review Run, PR feedback Watch, review command alias
EXIT: 0

# Task 01: The guides, the skill and the glossary name the resolved key and the Reviewed Head Record

## Overview

task_02 and task_03 change where a checkout's review record lives and what
the Delivery Retry's correction proof reads; this Task states that contract
first, in the same Pull Request. The review and deliver user
guides and the Roundfix skill's review and deliver references describe those
records (`docs/agents/cli.md`; ADR-0256 makes the skill files the covering
files of the `review` and `deliver` surfaces), and `CONTEXT.md` names the new
artifact. This Task writes `_techspec.md` → API Contract 5, raises the owned
skill's version with its record command, adds and revises the glossary terms
and re-records the Behavior Surface Record. It is verifiable on its own
through the phrases, the mirrors, the version record and the coverage
contract.

## Requirements

1. MUST add to `docs/user-guide/commands/review.md` and to
   `.agents/skills/roundfix/references/review.md`, directly after the
   sentence "Roundfix never reads another checkout's record.", the text of
   API Contract 5 for the review command, containing the phrases
   `named from the resolved checkout path` and `Reviewed Head Record`, the
   path `heads/<head commit>.json`, and the statement that a later round never
   replaces it and that a reused, blocked or omitted record writes none.
2. MUST rewrite, in `docs/user-guide/commands/deliver.md` and
   `.agents/skills/roundfix/references/deliver.md`, the moved-head
   `corrective-spec-required` paragraph and the matching row of the retry
   table to state API Contract 5 and API Contract 6 for the retry: the phrase
   `Reviewed Head Record of the parked candidate`; the widened path rule with
   the phrase `or among the paths its Tasks declared`, naming `creates:`,
   `interface:` and recorded paths read at the recorded revision; that review
   round 2 and the repository checks still follow; and that any other path
   still requires a corrective Spec, which the refusal names. MUST rewrite
   the last sentence of the archived-Specs paragraph of
   `docs/user-guide/commands/review.md` and
   `.agents/skills/roundfix/references/review.md` the same way. After this
   Task no copy of the four files says the correction "answers only the
   review in the archived Spec's own records".
3. MUST run `make skills-sync`, then
   `go test ./skills -run '^TestEveryOwnedSkillVersionIsRecorded$' -record-skill-versions`
   to raise and record the `roundfix` skill version. MUST NOT edit text
   inside any `### QA settlement` section, and MUST NOT change any other
   skill.
4. MUST add **Reviewed Head Record** to `CONTEXT.md` through
   `domain-modeling`, next to **Pre-PR Review Record**, linked to ADR-0272:
   the copy of a Pre-PR Review Record kept under the head it reviewed, written
   for every verdict that is not a reuse and never replaced by a later round
   at another head, which a Delivery Retry's correction proof reads for the
   parked candidate; `Avoid` synonyms such as round record or review history.
   MUST revise **Pre-PR Review Record** to say it is kept per checkout under
   its resolved path, with the phrase
   `kept per checkout under its resolved path`, linked to ADR-0272. Neither
   entry may reference a Spec.
5. MUST re-record the Behavior Surface Record with
   `go test -count=1 -tags docscontract ./internal/docscontract -run '^TestTheSkillCoverageMapIsCurrent$' -record-skill-coverage`;
   the Skill Coverage Map itself does not change.
6. MUST search the user guides, `CONTEXT.md`, the agent guides and the
   Roundfix skill references for wording that says the review directory is
   named from the checkout path as spelled, that a review record is replaced
   by every review in the checkout, or that the retry admits only paths under
   the archived Spec, and change any it finds in this Task; the search on
   `main` at v0.76.0 found only the sentences Requirements 1 and 2 change.

## Subtasks

- [ ] Add the review and deliver text to both guides and both skill references.
- [ ] Sync the mirror and record the version raise.
- [ ] Add and revise the glossary terms and re-record the Behavior Surface Record.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] The guides and both copies of the skill references carry the new phrases.
- [ ] The `SKILL.md` pair matches and the owned-skill version record passes.
- [ ] `CONTEXT.md` defines **Reviewed Head Record** and the revised **Pre-PR Review Record**, and the Skill Coverage Map is current.

- The Behavior Surface Record of the review and deliver guides — consumers: `./internal/docscontract`

## Result

Implementation and focused evidence for the acceptance criteria:

- The review and deliver user guides and both Roundfix skill reference copies
  now describe the resolved checkout key, `heads/<head commit>.json`, the
  Reviewed Head Record, the parked-candidate retry proof, declared paths
  (`creates:`, `interface:` and recorded paths at the recorded revision), the
  second review round and repository checks, and the corrective-Spec refusal.
  The focused wording sweep found no remaining copy of the superseded retry
  sentence.
- `make skills-sync` completed and kept the canonical and embedded Roundfix
  skill copies aligned. `go test ./skills -run
  '^TestEveryOwnedSkillVersionIsRecorded$' -record-skill-versions` completed
  with one passing package and recorded Roundfix `0.1.72`.
- `CONTEXT.md` now defines **Reviewed Head Record** beside **Pre-PR Review
  Record**, links both to ADR-0272, uses `kept per checkout under its resolved
  path`, and avoids Spec references and the prohibited synonyms. The required
  `go test -count=1 -tags docscontract ./internal/docscontract -run
  '^TestTheSkillCoverageMapIsCurrent$' -record-skill-coverage` completed with

# Task 02: The review record is keyed and compared by the resolved checkout

## Overview

`roundfix review` keys its record by the checkout root Git reports, the
resolved path, while the Delivery Retry's correction proof keys it by the item
worktree path the Delivery Queue recorded, which under a symbolic-linked
`worktree.location` is the link spelling, so the proof finds no record
(`_techspec.md` → Current behavior). This Task names the review directory from
the resolved checkout path and routes every repository comparison over a
review record or a disposition through one helper. It is verifiable on its
own in `internal/cli`: under a worktree root behind a symbolic link, review,
dispose `--fixed-by` and the proof called with the link spelling end with the
proof accepted.

## Requirements

1. MUST answer the Backlog Entry of 2026-10-13,
   [the review correction proof misses its record under a symlinked worktree root](references/2026-10-13-the-review-correction-proof-misses-its-record-under-a-symlinked-root.md),
   by implementing `_techspec.md` → API Contract 1 in `reviewCheckoutDir`
   with `roundconfig.CanonicalizeResolvedPath`, keeping the 16-character
   SHA-256 digest and the `pre-pr-review/<key>` layout.
2. MUST implement `_techspec.md` → API Contract 2: add the unexported
   `sameReviewCheckout(left, right string) bool` in `internal/cli/review.go`
   and use it at every repository comparison the sweep lists in
   `internal/cli/review.go`, `internal/cli/review_lineage.go`,
   `internal/cli/review_validation.go` and
   `internal/cli/deliver_review_correction.go`, including
   `reviewDispositionsAtHead` and the proof's record check: the twelve sites
   that compare a `Repository` field with `==` or `!=` or through
   `filepath.Clean`. None may remain in those four files; the emptiness check
   in `validateReviewRecord` is not a comparison and stays. Recorded `repository` values and ledger lines MUST NOT be
   rewritten.
3. MUST add `internal/cli/review_resolved_checkout_test.go` with the four
   tests `_techspec.md` → Testing Approach names for task_02, each calling
   `t.Parallel()` first, building a worktree root `<tmp>/link` that is a
   symbolic link to `<tmp>/real` and adding the item worktree with
   `git worktree add <tmp>/link/repo-slug/item`:
   - `TestReviewCheckoutDirIsOneForEverySpellingOfACheckout`: the link and the
     resolved spelling name one directory, equal to the digest of the resolved
     path (Invariant 1), and a sibling worktree names another (Invariant 2).
   - `TestTheCorrectionProofFindsTheRecordUnderASymlinkedWorktreeRoot`, with
     subtests `explicit-artifact-directory` and `default-artifact-directory`:
     `roundfix review` run in the item worktree with a fake reviewer raising
     one finding on `review.txt`, a commit changing only
     `docs/history/specs/0225-example/_prd.md`,
     `roundfix review dispose F1 --fixed-by <fix>`, then
     `ProveReviewCorrection` called with the link spelling, the slug
     `0225-example`, the reviewed head and the fix returns `Accepted: true`
     and no error.
   - `TestTheCorrectionProofRefusesACodeFixUnderASymlinkedWorktreeRoot`: the
     same flow with a fix that changes `review.txt`, which no Task of the
     archived Spec declares, is refused with a reason containing
     `changed path review.txt is outside the named archived Specs` (ADR-0165);
     the reason task_04 widens keeps that text as its prefix.
   - `TestTheCorrectionProofRefusesARecordOfAnotherCheckout`: a findings
     record naming a sibling worktree, placed in the item's review directory,
     is refused with a reason containing `review record must name the item worktree`.
4. MUST keep every existing test passing unchanged, including
   `deliver_review_correction_test.go`, `review_record_checkout_test.go`,
   `review_lineage_test.go` and `review_disposition_test.go`; a test that
   breaks is a defect in this Task, not a contract change.
5. MUST run the new tests once with `-race` (ADR-0259), give no wait a real
   time bound (ADR-0271), and MUST NOT change any command's output, flag,
   field or exit code, `internal/config`, or `internal/delivery`.

## Subtasks

- [ ] Add `sameReviewCheckout` and replace every repository comparison of the sweep.

- [ ] No lexical repository comparison remains in the four review files.

## Result

Implemented the Task 02 slice for Daemon Verification. `reviewCheckoutDir`
now hashes `roundconfig.CanonicalizeResolvedPath(checkoutRoot)` while retaining
its 16-character SHA-256 key and `pre-pr-review/<key>` layout.
`sameReviewCheckout` compares resolved paths at all twelve repository
comparison sites in the four declared review/proof sources. Repository fields,
ledger writes, the record schema, command output, flags and exit codes retain
their existing contracts; `internal/config` and `internal/delivery` are untouched.

The new `review_resolved_checkout_test.go` uses real Git worktrees under
`<tmp>/link/repo-slug/item`, with `link` pointing to `real`, temporary homes
and the existing per-test fake reviewer injection. All four top-level tests
and both Artifact Directory subtests call `t.Parallel()` first. No real-time
wait or timeout was added, and existing tests were not edited.

### Focused implementation checks

- Before the production change,
  `rtk proxy go test -count=1 -run 'TestReviewCheckoutDirIsOne|TestTheCorrectionProofFinds' ./internal/cli`
  exited 1: the linked spelling produced a different checkout digest, and both
  Artifact Directory modes failed with `read review correction record: open …:
  no such file or directory`.
- After the production change,
  `rtk proxy go test -count=1 -race -v -run 'TestReviewCheckoutDirIsOne|TestTheCorrectionProof' ./internal/cli`
  exited 0. All four named new tests and both Artifact Directory subtests
  reported PASS, with no race diagnostic.
- A focused Python harness extracted every top-level test name from the six
  existing suites below and invoked `go test -count=1 -run` with their exact
  anchored names. It selected 88 tests and exited 0 (`ok roundfix/internal/cli`).
  The command was:

  ```sh
  rtk proxy python3 - <<'PY'
  import re
  import subprocess
  from pathlib import Path
  files = ['review_test.go', 'review_record_checkout_test.go', 'review_lineage_test.go', 'review_disposition_test.go', 'deliver_review_correction_test.go', 'review_validation_test.go']
  names = []
  for name in files:
      names.extend(re.findall(r'^func (Test\w+)\(t \*testing.T\)', Path('internal/cli', name).read_text(), re.M))
  selector = '^(' + '|'.join(names) + ')$'
  print(f'Running {len(names)} existing tests from ' + ', '.join(files), flush=True)
  command = ['go', 'test', '-count=1', '-run', selector, './internal/cli']
  raise SystemExit(subprocess.run(command).returncode)
  PY
  ```

- Source inspection with
  `rtk proxy rg -n '\.Repository|sameReviewCheckout|reviewCheckoutDir' internal/cli/review.go internal/cli/review_lineage.go internal/cli/review_validation.go internal/cli/deliver_review_correction.go`
  showed all twelve comparisons routed through the helper and the unchanged
  repository emptiness check. A separate focused Python scan confirmed twelve
  helper call sites and zero lexical repository comparisons, exiting 0.
- `rtk proxy git diff --check` exited 0.

# Task 03: Each reviewed head keeps its record, and the proof reads the candidate's

## Overview

A checkout keeps one Pre-PR Review Record, so review round 2 at the fixed head
replaces the record of the parked candidate's findings, and the correction
proof then refuses (`_techspec.md` → Current behavior). This Task keeps a
Reviewed Head Record for every non-reused verdict, under
`heads/<head commit>.json` in the checkout's review directory, and makes the
proof read the candidate's. It is verifiable on its own in `internal/cli`:
under a worktree root behind a symbolic link, review, dispose `--fixed-by`,
review round 2 and the proof called with the link spelling end with the proof
accepted.

## Requirements

1. MUST answer the Backlog Entry of 2026-10-13,
   [a second review round overwrites the parked findings record](references/2026-10-13-review-round-two-overwrites-the-parked-findings-record.md),
   by implementing `_techspec.md` → API Contract 3 in `persistReviewRecord`
   through the unexported `reviewHeadRecordPath`: after the current record is
   written, a record that is not `reused` and whose outcome is `reviewed`,
   `findings`, `findings-dismissed` or `ceiling-closed` is written with the
   same bytes, atomically, to `heads/<head commit>.json`. A `blocked` or
   `omitted` record, a reused record and a head that is not 40 or 64
   lowercase hexadecimal characters write no head record and leave an
   existing one byte-identical.
2. MUST implement `_techspec.md` → API Contract 4 in `ProveReviewCorrection`
   through the unexported `readReviewRecordAtHead`: the candidate's head
   record, else the current record when it names the candidate. A missing
   record is a refusal with the existing reason
   `review record must name the item worktree and parked candidate with outcome findings`,
   not an error. Every other check of the proof and its reason stay as they
   are.
3. MUST keep `pre-pr-review.json` as the record the next round, reuse and
   `roundfix review dispose` read; MUST NOT add a flag, a record field or a
   gc rule.
4. MUST add `internal/cli/review_head_record_test.go` with the six tests
   `_techspec.md` → Testing Approach names for task_03, each calling
   `t.Parallel()` first, on the symbolic-linked worktree root fixture of
   task_02 where a worktree is needed:
   - `TestASecondReviewRoundKeepsTheFirstRoundsRecord`: after round 1 with a
     finding, a fix, dispose `--fixed-by` and round 2 with no findings, the
     candidate's head record still has outcome `findings` and the round-1
     `findingItems`, the fix's head record has outcome `reviewed`, and
     `pre-pr-review.json` names the fix.
   - `TestTheCorrectionProofAcceptsAfterASecondRoundUnderASymlinkedWorktreeRoot`:
     the same flow with a docs-only fix under `docs/history/specs/0225-example/`
     ends with the proof, called with the link spelling, accepted.
   - `TestAReusedBlockedOrOmittedReviewWritesNoReviewedHeadRecord`, with
     subtests `reused`, `blocked` and `omitted`: each leaves no new head record
     and the existing one byte-identical.
   - `TestTheCorrectionProofReadsTheCurrentRecordWithoutAHeadRecord`: a
     current findings record of the candidate with no `heads/` directory, as
     an item parked before this change has, is accepted.
   - `TestTheCorrectionProofRefusesWithoutARecordOfTheCandidate`: a current
     record of another head and no head record of the candidate are refused
     with the existing reason and no error.
   - `TestReviewHeadRecordPathRefusesANonCommitName`, with subtests `empty`,
     `traversal`, `uppercase` and `short`: each returns false.
5. MUST keep every existing test passing unchanged, including
   `deliver_review_correction_test.go`, `review_record_checkout_test.go`,
   `review_lineage_test.go`, `review_head_bound_test.go` and
   `review_disposition_test.go`, and task_02's tests.
6. MUST run the new tests once with `-race` (ADR-0259), give no wait a real
   time bound (ADR-0271), and MUST NOT change any command's output, flag,
   field or exit code, or `internal/delivery`.

## Subtasks

- [ ] Write the Reviewed Head Record in `persistReviewRecord`.
- [ ] Read the candidate's record in `ProveReviewCorrection`.
- [ ] Add the six tests.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Round 2 leaves the candidate's findings record readable, and the proof accepts after it.
- [ ] Reused, blocked and omitted records write no head record.
- [ ] An item parked before this change is still proved from its current record.

## Consumers

- Reviewed Head Record and the review correction proof's reader — consumers: `./internal/cli`

## Context

- instruction: `docs/adr/0272-a-review-record-survives-a-fix-and-a-correction-inside-the-archived-specs-declared-paths-returns-to-review.md`
- instruction: `docs/adr/0197-a-pre-pr-reviewer-lineage-spans-at-most-two-rounds.md`
- instruction: `internal/cli/review_lineage_test.go`
- instruction: `internal/cli/review_head_bound_test.go`
- instruction: `docs/user-guide/commands/review.md`
- instruction: `docs/user-guide/commands/deliver.md`
- interface: `internal/cli/review.go`
- interface: `internal/cli/deliver_review_correction.go`
- creates: `internal/cli/review_head_record_test.go`

## Verification

# Task 03: Each reviewed head keeps its record, and the proof reads the candidate's

## Overview

A checkout keeps one Pre-PR Review Record, so review round 2 at the fixed head
replaces the record of the parked candidate's findings, and the correction
proof then refuses (`_techspec.md` → Current behavior). This Task keeps a
Reviewed Head Record for every non-reused verdict, under
`heads/<head commit>.json` in the checkout's review directory, and makes the
proof read the candidate's. It is verifiable on its own in `internal/cli`:
under a worktree root behind a symbolic link, review, dispose `--fixed-by`,
review round 2 and the proof called with the link spelling end with the proof
accepted.

## Requirements

1. MUST answer the Backlog Entry of 2026-10-13,
   [a second review round overwrites the parked findings record](references/2026-10-13-review-round-two-overwrites-the-parked-findings-record.md),
   by implementing `_techspec.md` → API Contract 3 in `persistReviewRecord`
   through the unexported `reviewHeadRecordPath`: after the current record is
   written, a record that is not `reused` and whose outcome is `reviewed`,
   `findings`, `findings-dismissed` or `ceiling-closed` is written with the
   same bytes, atomically, to `heads/<head commit>.json`. A `blocked` or
   `omitted` record, a reused record and a head that is not 40 or 64
   lowercase hexadecimal characters write no head record and leave an
   existing one byte-identical.
2. MUST implement `_techspec.md` → API Contract 4 in `ProveReviewCorrection`
   through the unexported `readReviewRecordAtHead`: the candidate's head
   record, else the current record when it names the candidate. A missing
   record is a refusal with the existing reason
   `review record must name the item worktree and parked candidate with outcome findings`,
   not an error. Every other check of the proof and its reason stay as they
   are.
3. MUST keep `pre-pr-review.json` as the record the next round, reuse and
   `roundfix review dispose` read; MUST NOT add a flag, a record field or a
   gc rule.
4. MUST add `internal/cli/review_head_record_test.go` with the six tests
   `_techspec.md` → Testing Approach names for task_03, each calling
   `t.Parallel()` first, on the symbolic-linked worktree root fixture of
   task_02 where a worktree is needed:
   - `TestASecondReviewRoundKeepsTheFirstRoundsRecord`: after round 1 with a
     finding, a fix, dispose `--fixed-by` and round 2 with no findings, the
     candidate's head record still has outcome `findings` and the round-1
     `findingItems`, the fix's head record has outcome `reviewed`, and
     `pre-pr-review.json` names the fix.
   - `TestTheCorrectionProofAcceptsAfterASecondRoundUnderASymlinkedWorktreeRoot`:
     the same flow with a docs-only fix under `docs/history/specs/0225-example/`
     ends with the proof, called with the link spelling, accepted.
   - `TestAReusedBlockedOrOmittedReviewWritesNoReviewedHeadRecord`, with
     subtests `reused`, `blocked` and `omitted`: each leaves no new head record
     and the existing one byte-identical.
   - `TestTheCorrectionProofReadsTheCurrentRecordWithoutAHeadRecord`: a
     current findings record of the candidate with no `heads/` directory, as
     an item parked before this change has, is accepted.
   - `TestTheCorrectionProofRefusesWithoutARecordOfTheCandidate`: a current
     record of another head and no head record of the candidate are refused
     with the existing reason and no error.
   - `TestReviewHeadRecordPathRefusesANonCommitName`, with subtests `empty`,
     `traversal`, `uppercase` and `short`: each returns false.
5. MUST keep every existing test passing unchanged, including
   `deliver_review_correction_test.go`, `review_record_checkout_test.go`,
   `review_lineage_test.go`, `review_head_bound_test.go` and
   `review_disposition_test.go`, and task_02's tests, with one exception:
   `TestTheCorrectionProofRefusesARecordOfAnotherCheckout` in
   `internal/cli/review_resolved_checkout_test.go` replaces only the current
   record, and the proof now prefers the candidate's Reviewed Head Record. Its
   fixture MUST also write the sibling-checkout record as the candidate's
   Reviewed Head Record, so the test keeps refusing the record the proof
   reads; its assertion and refusal text stay unchanged.
6. MUST run the new tests once with `-race` (ADR-0259), give no wait a real
   time bound (ADR-0271), and MUST NOT change any command's output, flag,
   field or exit code, or `internal/delivery`.

## Subtasks

- [ ] Write the Reviewed Head Record in `persistReviewRecord`.
- [ ] Read the candidate's record in `ProveReviewCorrection`.
- [ ] Add the six tests.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Round 2 leaves the candidate's findings record readable, and the proof accepts after it.
- [ ] Reused, blocked and omitted records write no head record.
- [ ] An item parked before this change is still proved from its current record.

## Consumers

## Result

Implemented this Task's slice for Daemon Verification. Task status, Subtasks
and Acceptance Criteria checkboxes are unchanged; settlement belongs to the
Daemon.

`persistReviewRecord` encodes once, atomically writes the current record, and
then writes the identical bytes to `heads/<head commit>.json` for a non-reused
`reviewed`, `findings`, `findings-dismissed` or `ceiling-closed` verdict.
`reviewHeadRecordPath` admits only 40 or 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters.
Both record destinations use a temporary file, mode `0644`, and rename in a
`0755` directory. Reused, blocked, omitted and invalid-head records leave
existing head records untouched.

`ProveReviewCorrection` now uses `readReviewRecordAtHead` to prefer the parked
candidate's head record. When that file is absent, the current record is used
only if it names the candidate. Missing evidence refuses with the existing
record reason and no error; other read errors and proof checks retain their
behavior. The next round, reuse and disposition still read the current record.
The sibling-checkout refusal fixture now writes the sibling record to both
locations so its unchanged assertion covers the preferred record.

### Focused evidence

Before production changes,
`go test -count=1 -run 'TestASecondReviewRoundKeeps|TestTheCorrectionProofAcceptsAfterASecondRound' ./internal/cli`
exited `1`: the candidate's head record was absent after round 2, and the
proof refused with the existing record reason.

After the final production edit:

- `go test -race -count=1 -v -run 'Test(ASecondReviewRoundKeeps|TheCorrectionProofAcceptsAfterASecondRound|AReusedBlockedOrOmitted|TheCorrectionProofReadsTheCurrent|TheCorrectionProofRefusesWithoutARecord|ReviewHeadRecordPath)' ./internal/cli`
  exited `0`; all six required tests and their subtests passed under the race
  detector (`ok roundfix/internal/cli`, 2.616 s).
- `go test -count=1 -run '^Test(Review(RecordsLive|InOneCheckout|Dispose|IgnoresARecordAtTheShared|Lineage|RoundTwo|Ceiling|BlockedRoundTwo|Reports|KeepsStanding|IgnoresA|AsksAgain|NeverReuses|RecordRefuses|CorrectionRequires)|AReviewOnlyCorrection|ACorrection|TheCorrectionProof(Finds|RefusesA)|ReviewCheckoutDirIs)' ./internal/cli`
  exited `0` (`ok roundfix/internal/cli`, 3.901 s). This focused selection covers
  the affected existing checkout, disposition, lineage, head-bound and
  correction behavior, including task_02's sibling-checkout refusal.

| Acceptance criterion | Implementation and focused-check evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Round 2 preserves the candidate's findings and the proof accepts afterwards | `TestASecondReviewRoundKeepsTheFirstRoundsRecord` compares the candidate's full record, including its original findingItems, with round 1; confirms the fix's record and current record match round 2 with identical bytes; and checks file/directory permissions. `TestTheCorrectionProofAcceptsAfterASecondRoundUnderASymlinkedWorktreeRoot` accepts the docs-only correction using the link spelling after disposal and round 2. |
| Reused, blocked and omitted records write no head record | `TestAReusedBlockedOrOmittedReviewWritesNoReviewedHeadRecord` checks each exclusion against an existing head and a new head, preserving the former byte-identically and leaving the latter absent while still persisting the current record. `TestReviewHeadRecordPathRefusesANonCommitName` covers all four required invalid names, accepts SHA-1/SHA-256 names, and checks nonempty invalid names preserve existing head files. |
| An older parked item is still proved from its current record | `TestTheCorrectionProofReadsTheCurrentRecordWithoutAHeadRecord` accepts the symlinked correction with no heads directory and leaves that directory absent. `TestTheCorrectionProofRefusesWithoutARecordOfTheCandidate` confirms another current head or no current record produces the exact existing refusal reason without an error. |

All six new top-level tests call `t.Parallel()` first. No sleep, timer or
short real-time wait budget was added. Changes are confined to `review.go`,
`deliver_review_correction.go`, the new `review_head_record_test.go`, the
permitted fixture change in `review_resolved_checkout_test.go`, and this
Result section. No public flag, record field, output schema, exit code, gc
rule or `internal/delivery` file changed. Declared Task Verification and the
repository gates were not run; the Daemon owns them. No commit, push or Pull
Request was made. No follow-up implementation is included.

# Task 04: A code correction inside the archived Spec's declared paths returns to review

## Overview

ADR-0233's third retry rule admits a changed path only under an archived Spec
the blocker names, so a disposed code fix is refused even when the archived
Spec's own Task declared the file (`_techspec.md` → Current behavior, second
characterization). On 2026-10-13 the maintainer answered "Relaxar, com
limites": the retry also admits a path the archived Spec's Tasks declared.
This Task adds `spec.DeclaredTaskPaths` and makes the correction proof collect
each named Spec's declared paths at the recorded revision. It is verifiable on
its own: a disposed fix to a declared file is accepted, for a Spec archived as
a record and as a folder, and any other file is refused by name.

## Requirements

1. MUST implement `_techspec.md` → API Contract 6, refining ADR-0233 as
   ADR-0272 records: add `DeclaredTaskPaths(content []byte, taskFile string) ([]string, error)`
   in `internal/spec/declared_paths.go`, returning the clean, deduplicated
   `creates:` and `interface:` paths of one Task file's `## Context` and the
   paths of its `## Recorded paths` (`RecordedTaskPaths`), through the
   package's existing Task parser; and add the unexported
   `archivedSpecDeclaredPaths` in `internal/cli/deliver_review_correction.go`.
2. MUST read the declared paths from Git, never from the working tree: for a
   Legacy Archive Folder, its `task_*.md` files at the parked candidate; for an
   Archive Record, the `task_*.md` files under its `source` at its
   `source_revision`, recovered with `spec.EnsureSourceRevision` when the
   commit is missing locally (ADR-0261). Unreadable or unparsable Task files
   refuse with the reason
   `declared paths of archived Spec <slug> are unavailable: <cause>`.
3. MUST admit a changed path that lies under the Spec's archive path, as
   today, or equals one of its declared paths exactly, and refuse the first
   other path in the order Git lists it with the reason
   `changed path <path> is outside the named archived Specs and the paths their Tasks declared`.
   The descent, disposition and record checks and their reasons MUST stay as
   task_02 and task_03 leave them, and the accepted item MUST still return to
   `reviewing` (round 2, archive, repository gate and required checks follow,
   unchanged in `internal/delivery`).
4. MUST add `internal/spec/declared_paths_test.go` with
   `TestDeclaredTaskPathsReadsCreatesInterfaceAndRecordedPaths`, and
   `internal/cli/review_declared_correction_test.go` with the five tests
   `_techspec.md` → Testing Approach names for task_04, each calling
   `t.Parallel()` first, on temporary repositories with a fake reviewer:
   - `TestACodeCorrectionInsideTheDeclaredPathsReturnsToReview`, subtests
     `archive-record` (the Spec's folder committed with a Task declaring
     `interface: review.txt`, then archived as a record whose
     `source_revision` is that commit) and `legacy-folder` (the Task file
     inside `docs/history/specs/0225-example/` at the candidate): review with
     one finding on `review.txt`, a fix to `review.txt`, dispose
     `--fixed-by`, and the proof returns `Accepted: true`.
   - `TestARecordedPathCountsAsDeclared`: a path only under the Task's
     `## Recorded paths` is admitted.
   - `TestACodeCorrectionOutsideTheDeclaredPathsNamesTheFirstPath`: a fix that
     changes `review.txt` and `other.txt` is refused with the reason naming
     `other.txt`.
   - `TestDeclaredPathsAreReadAtTheRecordedRevision`: a correction that also
     edits the archived Task file, or a working-tree copy, to declare
     `other.txt` does not admit `other.txt` (Invariant 5).
   - `TestUnreadableDeclaredPathsRefuseTheCorrection`: a record whose
     `source_revision` cannot be read, with no remote, is refused with the
     unavailable reason and no error.
5. MUST keep every existing test passing unchanged, including
   `TestACorrectionOutsideTheArchivedSpecIsRefused` and task_02's
   `TestTheCorrectionProofRefusesACodeFixUnderASymlinkedWorktreeRoot`, whose
   asserted reasons stay prefixes of the new one.
6. MUST run the new tests once with `-race` (ADR-0259), give no wait a real
   time bound (ADR-0271), reach no network, and MUST NOT change
   `internal/delivery`, the review command's corrective-Spec line, or any
   command's flag or exit code.

## Subtasks

- [ ] Add `spec.DeclaredTaskPaths` and its test.
- [ ] Collect the declared paths at the recorded revision in the proof and widen the path rule.
- [ ] Add the five correction tests.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A disposed fix to a declared file returns to review, for a record and for a folder.
- [ ] Any other changed path is refused by name, and the declared set cannot be widened by the correction.
- [ ] Unreadable declared paths refuse without an error.

## Consumers

- `spec.DeclaredTaskPaths` and the review correction proof's path rule — consumers: `./internal/cli`, `./internal/spec`

## Result

Implemented the declared-path correction slice. `spec.DeclaredTaskPaths`
uses the existing Task parser to collect `creates:` and `interface:` entries
and `RecordedTaskPaths`, returning clean, sorted, unique repository paths.
Instruction and deletion entries do not grant correction paths; invalid Task
content or unsafe recorded paths return an error.

The proof first applies the existing archive-path rule. When a changed path
lies outside those archives, it reads each named archive's form at the parked
candidate and collects the declared set once. Legacy Task files are read at
that candidate; Archive Record Task files are read under `source` at
`source_revision`, using `spec.EnsureSourceRevision` and the configured
delivery remote for missing objects. All Task listings and contents come
from Git. Exact declared-path matches join the existing archive-path rule.
The first other changed path is refused in Git's listing order with the
required extended reason. Unavailable declarations become a refusal with
the outside-archive path context, their cause and no returned error. A
correction confined to archive paths needs no Task declarations and retains
its existing acceptance even when the record's source revision is absent.

Acceptance evidence from focused implementation checks:

| Acceptance criterion | Evidence |
| --- | --- |
| A disposed fix to a declared file returns to review, for a record and for a folder. | `TestACodeCorrectionInsideTheDeclaredPathsReturnsToReview`, subtests `archive-record` and `legacy-folder`, exercises public review/dispose commands with a fake reviewer in real temporary symlinked Git worktrees and asserts `Accepted: true`. `TestARecordedPathCountsAsDeclared` also admits a recorded-only path in both forms. |
| Any other changed path is refused by name, and the declared set cannot be widened by the correction. | `TestACodeCorrectionOutsideTheDeclaredPathsNamesTheFirstPath` asserts the exact refusal naming `other.txt` when the correction also changes `review.txt`. `TestDeclaredPathsAreReadAtTheRecordedRevision` refuses `other.txt` for both archive forms with committed and uncommitted Task edits, including an uncommitted source-folder copy for records. |
| Unreadable declared paths refuse without an error. | `TestUnreadableDeclaredPathsRefuseTheCorrection` covers a missing source revision with no remote and unparsable Task content in both archive forms. Its proof helper requires no returned error, and each case asserts the unavailable reason with a cause. |

$ git show fd2f46f200f2fffe6181e7fb6dcf5342b4a60e22:docs/user-guide/commands/deliver.md
### deliver

```bash
roundfix deliver plan [--json] [<slug>...]
roundfix deliver start [--max-duration <duration>] [--max-retries <n>] [--max-tokens <n>] <slug>...
roundfix deliver status
roundfix deliver resume
roundfix deliver retry <slug>
roundfix deliver stop
```

Run `roundfix deliver plan` before recording a queue. With explicit slugs it
keeps their order; without slugs it reports every active Spec. Text output is
tab-separated:

- `spec` rows give the slug, `approved` or `blocked` verdict, unfinished and
  total Task counts, and authorization or strict Spec-check reasons.
- `shared` rows name production Go `interface:` paths a later Spec also shares
  with an earlier Spec in the plan.
- `backlog`, `finding`, and `inbox` rows list repository intent that is not
  approved to run. Backlog and finding rows carry their frontmatter status;
  inbox rows use `-`.

`--json` emits one `roundfix-deliver-plan/v1` document with the same Specs,
verdicts, reasons, shared premises, Task counts, and intent. Exit `0` means
every reported Spec is approved, exit `1` means at least one is blocked, and
exit `2` means usage or preflight failed. The plan opens no Run Database,
creates no worktree, and writes no file. It reports authority but never grants
implementation or delivery authority. A `shared` row predicts the later
item's `premise-changed` warning after the earlier item merges; it never blocks
the Spec or stops a queue.

Creates and operates a durable, ordered queue of Specs. Each item advances
from its Run to merge in this order: Run, pre-PR review, archive on the branch,
repository gate, push, pull request, Pull Request Record Commit, current-head
checks, and squash merge.
When the configured pre-PR review is `none`, the queue records that omission
and archives after the Run's QA gate.

After it opens the pull request and before it judges checks, the Delivery Queue
records the pull request number in the Archive Record in a Pull Request Record
Commit. The commit changes the record's `pull_request` field and the queue
pushes it before judging the pull request checks.

A Spec can name prerequisite Specs in its Task Graph manifest (`_tasks.md`):

```yaml
requires:
  - 0192-first-prerequisite
  - 0193-second-prerequisite
```

`requires` is optional. It must be a list of distinct, non-empty strings and
cannot name the Spec itself. `deliver start` loads every queued Spec, then
refuses unknown prerequisites or cycles among queued Specs with exit `2`
before checking delivery authorization or recording a queue. A prerequisite
must exist in the Specs Root or its resolved archive root; it need not be in
the queue.

`deliver start` refuses a queue whose Spec requires a cancelled Spec, and
`deliver retry` refuses a cancelled Spec.

Before creating an item's worktree, the owner fetches the delivery remote's
default branch and reads `requires` from that refreshed branch. A prerequisite
is met only when its archived `_prd.md` exists there. An archive present only
in your checkout or an unmerged branch does not count. With no unmet
prerequisites, the item starts as usual.

When every unmet prerequisite is an item in the queue that is neither parked
nor merged, the dependent item waits at `queued`; the owner logs the wait and
moves on. Otherwise it parks as `prerequisite-unmerged: <slug>, …` without
creating a worktree. `deliver status` classifies this park as `dependency` and
points to the prerequisite's retry when that prerequisite is parked, or asks
you to deliver or merge it when it is outside the queue.

At the start of every owner pass and after any item merges, the owner returns
a dependency park to `queued` when all its prerequisites are met, clears the
blocker and leaves the retry count unchanged. When every item is parked, the
owner exits; retrying a prerequisite restarts it. You can also run
`roundfix deliver retry <slug>` on the dependent item to return it to `queued`
without a worktree, revalidation or carry-forward. The owner checks its
prerequisites again before starting it.

Each queued Spec runs in its own linked worktree under `worktree.location`.
After the prerequisite check and before authorization or readiness checks,
`deliver start` reads local item branches named
`roundfix/deliver-<slug>-<16 lowercase hex digits>`. It compares them with the
local `<remote>/<default>` ref without fetching. With exactly one branch
holding commits that ref lacks, it records the new queue and prints on stdout:

```text
Continuing item branch <branch> for <slug>
```

The owner checks again after fetching the default branch and records that
branch on the new item, reusing its worktree and completed work. The item
starts at `queued`, as any new item does; Roundfix does not merge the default
branch into it at start. With no branch holding work, it creates a new item
branch from the refreshed default branch.

With two or more item branches with commits the default branch lacks, start
exits `2` before recording a queue. The reason names every branch in sorted
order:

```text
Spec "0300-example" has 2 item branches with commits origin/main lacks: roundfix/deliver-0300-example-1111111111111111, roundfix/deliver-0300-example-2222222222222222; delete every branch but the one to continue, then run roundfix deliver start again
```

`roundfix deliver` never switches, resets or cleans your checkout, and it does not need the checkout to be clean.
A parked item keeps its worktree, and `deliver status` prints that path. On
resume, Roundfix recreates a missing worktree from its recorded branch; if the
branch is missing too, it parks the item as `item-worktree-missing` instead of
replaying the stage. After an item merges, its worktree and local item branch
are removed. Before that removal, cleanup refreshes the default branch from the delivery remote.
Roundfix then releases every terminal Run of the merged Spec that it can prove
is represented at the recorded merged head. A Run it cannot prove stays in
place, and `deliver status` names the Run and its reason in the item's cleanup
warning. This cleanup proves a squash merge onto a default branch that moved
during the item by comparing the merge commit with Git's merge of the candidate
into its first parent.

The archive commit also carries the promoted copies and every relinked file
named by the Archive Record. The queue accepts a relinked file only when it is
outside the archived folder and History Root, existed at the parent, and its
bytes equal the archive's rewrite of the parent's bytes under the same citation
moves; an extra or altered relinked file fails the archive proof.

A start requires every named Spec's committed authorization to grant
`implement`, `commit`, `push`, `pull_request`, and `merge`. If any Spec lacks
one of those operations, `deliver start` exits `2`, names every refused Spec
and its reasons, points to `roundfix deliver plan`, and records no queue. A
strict Spec-check finding appears in the plan but does not refuse start; the
queue revalidates that Spec against its own starting main.

After checking delivery authorization, `deliver start` checks the `gh` and
`remote` readiness lines before opening the Run Database. Any `failed` finding
refuses with exit `2`: the `Preflight failed` block says `Delivery Queue
cannot publish from this machine:` and lists one reason per finding, including
its `DR-` code and next action. It creates no Delivery Queue and starts no
owner. A `warn`, such as `DR-GH-UNREACHABLE` on an offline machine, is silent
and does not refuse start.

For example, a missing GitHub login produces:

```text
Preflight failed

Reason:
  Delivery Queue cannot publish from this machine:
  gh: DR-GH-UNAUTHENTICATED: gh has no account for github.com; next: gh auth login --hostname github.com

No side effects:
  Roundfix did not create a Run, fetch Review Source issues, start an Agent, commit, or push.

Usage:
  Run 'roundfix deliver start --help' for usage.
```

A Spec that changes no Governed Path records `paths: []`; that explicit empty
list grants the listed operations and bounds no Governed Path.

Use `--max-duration <duration>` with a positive Go duration to set the queue
deadline, and use `--max-retries <n>` with an integer of at least `1` to limit
retries per item. Omitted limits are recorded as `none`. Start and status print
the recorded values as:

```text
Limits: deadline <RFC 3339 UTC|none>, retries per item <n|none>, concurrency 1, tokens <n|none>
```

`deliver start` prints the limits line after any continuation lines.
`deliver status` prints usage immediately after it, summing every Run recorded for queue items, including
Runs from earlier retries:

```text
Limits: deadline none, retries per item none, concurrency 1, tokens none
Usage: 5639755 tokens from 1 of 1 prompt(s) across 1 Run(s); cost not reported
```

With unreported prompts the line is `Usage: none reported by <n> prompt(s)
across <k> Run(s); cost not reported`. A queue with no linked Runs prints
`Usage: no Runs recorded`. Linked Runs with no usage rows print `no prompts
recorded` across their Run count. A Run in progress contributes once the queue
records it at Run end. Reported cost is grouped by currency; Roundfix computes
no prices. See [Token usage](../usage.md#token-usage).

At or after the deadline, the owner parks each item that has not started as
`queue-deadline`; an item that has started continues. A `queue-deadline` item
cannot be retried. Record a new queue for the remaining Specs instead. When an
item reaches its retry limit, `deliver retry` refuses the next retry and leaves
the item unchanged.

After the item worktree is created or continued and before the first Run,
Roundfix runs the strict Spec Consistency Check in the worktree. A finding
parks the item as `revalidation-failed: <code>, <code>` before any Run starts.

Revalidation also compares the Spec's declared production Go `interface:`
paths with the merge commits of earlier queue items. An overlap records
`premise-changed: <path>, <path> (merge <sha>, <sha>)` as a warning and the item
continues to its Run. `deliver status` prints `Warning: <slug> <warning>` after
the item rows, and the delivery console log prints `roundfix: warning: Delivery
